DECISION F108 D3 — T003b'S FIRST ROUND WIRES ONLY THE BUILDER REPAIR-DIFF BRANCH; A NEW STANDALONE RENDERING HELPER CARRIES THE THRESHOLD/SELECTION LOGIC; full_ref IS A DESCRIPTIVE LABEL, NOT YET A PERSISTED PATH; REVIEWER-SIDE WIRING AND DISK CACHING ARE DEFERRED. THE PROBLEM: DECISION F108 D2 named `pingpong_loop.py`'s `compose_builder_prompt`/`compose_reviewer_prompt` diff-inclusion branches as T003b's targets in general terms but left the exact call-site precedence, the new threshold constant's value, and the caching/persistence question undecided — `.agent/plan.md`'s own Risks section flags exactly this ("the round inspects the exact call-site precedence... before extending it"). CHOSEN: (1) a new pure function `render_tiered_diff_text(diff_text, file_refs, call_fn, *, threshold_chars, full_ref)` in `artifact_summary.py` sections the diff (`section_diff`), generates via `generate_artifact_summary`, selects via `select_relevant_sections`, and renders a "## Current Staged Diff (summarized)" text, or "" at/under `threshold_chars` (the caller's signal to keep its existing flat-cap behavior). (2) a new pure gating helper `_builder_tiered_diff_text(repair_diff, findings, is_resumed, call_fn_factory, *, threshold_chars, full_ref)` in `pingpong_loop.py` fixes the precedence this round inspects: a resumed session (`is_resumed`) or no findings or a diff at/under threshold always returns "", so `compose_builder_prompt`'s existing `resume_hunks_text` precedence (DECISION F106 D1(b)) is preserved untouched and `summary_call_fn`'s Ollama-availability resolve is never spent when its result would be discarded — `call_fn_factory` is called (`summary_call_fn` itself, unapplied) only inside the branch that needs it. (3) `compose_builder_prompt` gains one new keyword parameter, `tiered_diff_text: str = ""`, checked in a new `elif tiered_diff_text:` branch between the existing `if resume_hunks_text:` and `elif safe_diff and findings:` — same segment name/rank (`builder_staged_diff`, JOB_CONTEXT) the flat-cap branch already uses, mirroring exactly how `resume_hunks_text` itself was added (round 12, DECISION F106 D1(b)); an empty string always falls through to the unchanged flat-cap branch, so every existing golden-test shape (`minimal`/`scope_task`/`staged`/`full`/`resumed`) is untouched. (4) the new threshold constant, `_OVERSIZED_DIFF_THRESHOLD_CHARS`, is set equal to the existing `_REPAIR_DIFF_CAP` (20000) — tiering activates exactly where the flat truncation used to activate, so this round literally REPLACES `_REPAIR_DIFF_CAP`'s truncation behavior for oversized diffs rather than adding a second, disconnected magic number. (5) `full_ref` this round is the descriptive string `f"repair diff, round {round_num} (F108: not yet persisted to evidence)"` — honest about NOT being a real path yet, since no artifact file is written to disk this round (T001's `save_summary`/`load_cached_summary` file-based caching stays unwired here); a later round persists the diff to evidence and passes a real path, which is WHEN the feature's literal "reference path present" Done condition is met for this call site. ALTERNATIVES CONSIDERED: (a) wire disk caching plus a real evidence path in the same round — rejected, too large for the 8-gate budget on top of the compose_builder_prompt/call-site changes and untested precedent (no existing call site writes ephemeral diff text to evidence for hashing); (b) wire `compose_reviewer_prompt`'s three diff branches in the same round — rejected for the same size reason; deferred to a follow-up round, tracked in `.agent/plan.md`. WHAT REMAINS FOR F108's DONE CONDITION: reviewer-side wiring (three cap sites: `_REVIEWER_DIFF_CAP`, `_REVIEWER_SCOPED_DIFF_CAP`, and the unscoped/scoped precedence already established in `compose_reviewer_prompt`), a real persisted `full_ref` path (disk caching wired), and the long-artifact fixture plus size-comparison recorded as the feature's own acceptance evidence — all tracked as open items in `.agent/plan.md`'s Next Steps, not claimed done here. HOW TO REVERSE: delete `render_tiered_diff_text`, `_builder_tiered_diff_text`, the `tiered_diff_text` parameter and its call site, and their tests; `compose_builder_prompt`'s remaining branches are byte-for-byte what they were before. WHAT IT COSTS TO BE WRONG: if the reviewer-side wiring later finds a different precedent shape is needed, the cost is one more standalone tested function plus one more parameter — the builder side stays correct and inert-safe (a "" `tiered_diff_text` is always a legal, already-tested input) regardless.

DECISION F108 D4 — REVIEWER-SIDE TIERING WIRES ONLY THE SCOPED BRANCH (`reviewer_focused_diff`), USING THE SCOPE PACKET'S OWN `changed_files` AS file_refs; THE FALLBACK BRANCH AND `diff_summary` STAY UNTOUCHED. THE PROBLEM: unlike the builder's repair-diff branch, `compose_reviewer_prompt` has no `findings`-shaped narrowing signal available at every call site -- `scope_packet` is built by `_build_runtime_scope_packet` and is `None` only when `safe_diff`/`staged_files` was empty or diff-parsing raised (a rare fallback), so the fallback branch (`elif safe_diff:` at `_REVIEWER_DIFF_CAP`) has no reliable file list to narrow against, and the reviewer's job on that path is to see the WHOLE diff regardless -- tiering it against an empty or absent file set would silently drop L2 detail the reviewer still needs, which `select_relevant_sections`' own design (T003a, round 6: "never a silent fallback to everything, which would defeat the size reduction the whole feature exists for") makes a real risk if misapplied here rather than a safe default. CHOSEN: the scope packet's `changed_files` field (independently confirmed at `_build_runtime_scope_packet`, `pingpong_loop.py:1277`: every file the just-computed safe diff touches, sorted, the SAME file set `section_diff` would section the diff into) is exactly the right `file_refs` for the scoped branch -- using it selects EVERY section the diff actually contains, dropping none, so tiering only ever compresses an oversized section's TEXT, never the reviewer's FILE coverage. This differs from the builder's repair-diff case (DECISION F108 D3), where `file_refs` = only the files named by open findings -- a genuine, intentional narrowing appropriate there because the builder is told to fix ONLY those findings; the reviewer has no such narrower mandate, so its `file_refs` set is deliberately the full changed-file list, never a subset. A new gating helper, `_reviewer_tiered_diff_text(safe_diff, scope_packet, is_resumed, call_fn_factory, *, threshold_chars, full_ref)`, mirrors `_builder_tiered_diff_text`'s short-circuit shape (resumed session / no `scope_packet` / under threshold all return "" before any provider resolve) and derives `file_refs` from `scope_packet["changed_files"]`. `compose_reviewer_prompt` gains one new parameter, `tiered_diff_text: str = ""`, checked in a new `elif tiered_diff_text:` branch inside the EXISTING `if scoped:` block, between `if resume_hunks_text:` and `elif safe_diff:` -- same segment name/rank (`reviewer_focused_diff`, STEERING) the flat-cap branch already uses, mirroring `resume_hunks_text`'s own precedent there (round 12, DECISION F106 D1(b)) exactly as DECISION F108 D3 mirrored it on the builder side; the fallback branch's `elif safe_diff:`/`elif diff_summary:` chain is NOT touched, so `tiered_diff_text` is inert whenever `scoped` is false. New threshold constant `_OVERSIZED_REVIEWER_SCOPED_DIFF_THRESHOLD_CHARS = _REVIEWER_SCOPED_DIFF_CAP` (12000), mirroring D3's own "deliberately equal to the cap it replaces" pattern. `full_ref` stays the same descriptive-label shape D3 chose (`f"reviewer diff, round {round_num} (F108: not yet persisted to evidence)"`) -- real persistence is still T003c, unchanged from D3. ALTERNATIVES CONSIDERED: (a) tier the fallback branch too, using `prior_findings` as `file_refs` on repair rounds and skipping tiering on a true first-pass review -- rejected this round: the fallback branch is reached only when `_build_runtime_scope_packet` itself failed, an already-degraded path, and adding a second helper with different narrowing semantics (findings-scoped, like the builder) beside a first-scoped one (file-list-scoped) in the same round risked exactly the kind of design confusion this DECISION exists to avoid; deferred, tracked in `.agent/plan.md`. (b) tier using `prior_findings` instead of `changed_files` on the scoped branch too, for symmetry with the builder -- rejected: `scoped` is true for nearly every non-empty diff in production (`scope_packet` is built whenever `safe_diff and staged_files`, independent of `prior_findings`/repair status), so narrowing to `prior_findings` would silently drop L2 coverage for files with no open finding on the FIRST review pass of every job, which is precisely the safety regression D4's THE PROBLEM section above warns against. WHAT REMAINS: the fallback branch, real evidence persistence (`full_ref`, T003c), disk caching (T003c), and the long-artifact fixture/size comparison (T003d) -- unchanged from DECISION F108 D3's own list, plus the fallback-branch deferral this DECISION adds. HOW TO REVERSE: delete `_reviewer_tiered_diff_text`, the `tiered_diff_text` parameter and its call site, and their tests; `compose_reviewer_prompt`'s remaining branches are byte-for-byte what they were before. WHAT IT COSTS TO BE WRONG: if `changed_files` later proves too wide (e.g. a future caller wants genuine narrowing on the scoped branch), the cost is one parameter change to the helper -- the segment-level wiring inside `compose_reviewer_prompt` does not change, since it only ever sees the caller's already-rendered text.

DECISION F108 D5 — T003c PERSISTS THE TIERED DIFF UNDER THE RUN'S OWN TRACE DIRECTORY (`_pingpong_runs_dir()/<run_id>/calls/<role>/round-NN/tiered_diff.diff`), REUSING THE EXISTING `call_evidence_dir` NAMING SHAPE FOR A NEW `kind`, NOT `task_runs/<task_id>/` -- WHICH BELONGS TO A SEPARATE, LATER JOB-EVIDENCE-AGGREGATION LAYER THIS FEATURE DOES NOT TOUCH. THE PROBLEM: T003c needs a REAL, resolvable `full_ref` path (the feature's literal Done condition, "the reference path present") plus T001's hash-invalidated caching wired at both call sites; `run_pingpong` itself has no direct access to `task_runs/<task_id>/` -- that tree is assembled by `job_evidence.py` from named, explicitly-collected artifacts (`result.diff`, `safe.diff`, `stream_artifacts.json`, `provider_evidence.json`) AFTER a run completes, and is independently validated by `artifact_contract_gate.py` (`check_stream_artifacts`, the unreferenced-`result.diff` check) and `change_provenance_gate.py` (globs `task_runs/*/safe.diff` for provenance coverage) -- both read by full-file inspection this round, both scanning ONLY their own known filenames, neither scanning the tree generally for "unexpected" files. Writing a brand-new artifact type into `task_runs/` would need its OWN wiring into `job_evidence.py`'s collection step to ever surface in a packaged evidence bundle, and skipping that wiring while still writing into that tree risks a silently-orphaned artifact those two gates never account for -- a second, separate integration surface this DECISION explicitly defers. CHOSEN: `_pingpong_runs_dir()/<run_id>/` is the tree `run_pingpong` ALREADY owns directly (`result.diff`, `prompt_trace.jsonl`, `result.json` all live there today), and `calls/<role>/round-NN/<kind>` is ALREADY its own established per-round-per-role convention (`call_evidence_dir`, `failure_postmortem.py:755`, used today for provider-call post-mortems). `render_tiered_diff_text` gains one new optional parameter, `artifact_path: Path | None = None` -- `None` (the default, what T003b's own landed tests still pass) keeps the function's ORIGINAL stateless contract exactly; given a real path, it writes `diff_text` there (parent dirs created), checks T001's `load_cached_summary` before any provider call, and calls `save_summary` after a fresh generation, so a byte-identical diff recomputed later (a resumed job, a retried round) is a cache HIT, never a second provider call. `_builder_tiered_diff_text`/`_reviewer_tiered_diff_text` each gain the SAME optional `artifact_path` param, purely forwarded. Both `run_pingpong` call sites now compute a real path -- `_pingpong_runs_dir() / result.run_id / "calls" / "builder-or-reviewer" / f"round-{round_num:02d}" / "tiered_diff.diff"` -- and pass `full_ref=str(that_path)`, so the rendered "Full diff:" line names a path that genuinely exists on disk the moment the round runs, discharging the feature's own Done-condition wording for both call sites at once. ALTERNATIVES CONSIDERED: (a) wire the artifact into `task_runs/` and `job_evidence.py`'s collection step in the same round -- rejected, a second cross-cutting integration surface on top of an already substantial round, and the two contract gates read this round give no evidence it is SAFE without also reading `job_evidence.py`'s own collection logic in full, which this round did not do. (b) skip caching, persist the path only -- rejected: T001's cache machinery already exists, is already tested, and the marginal cost of wiring it here (a `load_cached_summary`/`save_summary` pair around the existing `generate_artifact_summary` call) is small next to the value of never re-spending a provider call on an unchanged diff. WHAT REMAINS: T003d, the long-artifact fixture and size-comparison recording -- the feature's own DONE-condition evidence, still open; T003b-iii (the reviewer's fallback branch) stays deferred per DECISION F108 D4, unchanged. HOW TO REVERSE: drop `artifact_path` back to unconditionally `None` at both call sites (or delete the parameter and its cache-check branch entirely); `render_tiered_diff_text`'s stateless behavior for every EXISTING caller is unaffected either way, since the parameter is additive and defaults to today's behavior. WHAT IT COSTS TO BE WRONG: if `task_runs/` later proves the correct home after all (e.g. a future feature wants the tiered diff inside the packaged evidence bundle), the cost is a second write plus the `job_evidence.py` wiring this round explicitly deferred -- the `_pingpong_runs_dir()` copy this round lands is not wasted, since `call_evidence_dir`'s own post-mortem consumers already read that same tree.

DECISION F108 D6 — T003D'S FIXTURE DRIVES `run_pingpong` END TO END WITH A MONKEYPATCHED `_apply_fake_builder_changes`, NOT A GOAL-STRING-INFLATED `FakeProvider` OR A STATEFUL `claude-cli` BASH FIXTURE. THE PROBLEM: T003d (plan.md's own Risk note) requires a fixture that exercises the REAL call sites inside `run_pingpong` -- not `render_tiered_diff_text` in isolation -- and asserts the actually-SENT composed prompt is an order of magnitude smaller than the raw diff. The obvious route, inflating `FakeProvider`'s own `goal` argument so `_apply_fake_builder_changes`'s `content += f"\n\n<!-- Remedy: {goal} -->\n"` line writes a large staged file, is a dead end: `compose_builder_prompt`/`compose_reviewer_prompt` also embed the full `goal` text verbatim in their own "Goal" segment regardless of tiering, so inflating `goal` inflates the composed prompt by the SAME amount it inflates the raw diff and the order-of-magnitude comparison the DONE condition asks for becomes unmeasurable by construction. CHOSEN: `FakeProvider(builder_files=["big.py"], fail_on_round=1, pass_on_round=2)` drives exactly two rounds (round 1 needs_repair with one finding on `big.py`, round 2 pass -- the existing `TestRepairRoundGetsDiff` pattern), with `max_rounds=2, repair_rounds=2`; the test's own `monkeypatch.setattr(pingpong_loop, "_apply_fake_builder_changes", ...)` replaces ONLY the fake-content-authoring internal for the duration of the test with a version that writes a large, deterministic, goal-INDEPENDENT string (~86000 characters, no provider call, no network) into `big.py`, so the raw diff is large while the composed prompt's "Goal" segment stays a short literal goal string; `monkeypatch.setattr(pingpong_loop, "summary_call_fn", lambda: fake_call_fn)` makes the tiered summary itself deterministic and offline, mirroring round 7's and round 9's own fake-provider JSON-fixture pattern. The reviewer's own scoped-diff site fires in round 1 (its gate does not depend on repair at all, only on `is_resumed=False` and a non-empty safe diff); the builder's own repair-diff site fires in round 2 (`repair_diff` is computed from round 1's `result.staged_files`, and round 2's `findings` come from round 1's one real `ReviewFinding`) -- both are DECISION-independent facts of the already-landed round-9 wiring, re-read from `pingpong_loop.py`'s exact current bytes before this DECISION was written, not assumed. The test reads back the two real persisted `tiered_diff.diff` artifact files at their real, deterministic paths and compares each one's length against its matching `result.prompt_traces` entry's real `.prompt_chars` field (the exact composed-prompt length `run_pingpong` actually sent), asserting `prompt_chars < raw_len / 10` at both call sites -- the feature's own "enters a follow-up prompt... at a fraction of its size" DONE-condition wording, measured on the real call sites the plan.md Risk note demands. ALTERNATIVES CONSIDERED: (a) a stateful `fake_claude_builder_bin`/`fake_claude_reviewer_bin` bash-script pair (the existing claude-cli fixture family) -- rejected as heavier for no added correctness: no existing reviewer fixture is call-count-aware (round 1 fail, round 2 pass), so this route would add a small state-file protocol to a bash script for a property `FakeProvider`'s own `fail_on_round`/`pass_on_round` constructor args already give for free. (b) drive both call sites through `render_tiered_diff_text` directly, as round 7's own fixture test does -- rejected, this is exactly the isolation plan.md's Risk note names as insufficient for T003d's own DONE condition ("not just `render_tiered_diff_text` in isolation"). WHAT REMAINS: the integration gate and the closure sequence -- both plan.md `Next Steps` items, unaffected by this DECISION. HOW TO REVERSE: delete the new test class and its two monkeypatches; nothing else in `pingpong_loop.py` or `artifact_summary.py` is touched by this round, so no production code needs unwinding. WHAT IT COSTS TO BE WRONG: if the ~86000-character deterministic fixture string ever proves too small against a future threshold change, the cost is raising one literal in the test, not a design change -- the monkeypatch shape itself (goal-independent content, both real call sites exercised, real `prompt_traces` read back) is the part this DECISION commits to, not the exact byte count.

R-1320 READ AGAIN at F055's round 7 gate, by the reviewer: it is a defect of the product. When two runtime-flow checks race for one port, the loser's application dies on "Address already in use", `_wait_ready` has already seen the winner's application answer, and `port_listener_in_session` cannot read the dead process's sockets, so `_confirm_app_owns_port` in `packages/orchestration/dod_runners.py` goes on "without that proof" and the check passes on an application it did not start — the very case R-1251 was repaired for, left open where the owner is unreadable. The test that R-1320 names is right and catches it in about half of its serial runs. Owner: F055, by DECISION F055 D4.

- R-1331 — Medium, amend1015f: A RETRY OF A JOB WHOSE TASK ENDED BLOCKED RUNS NOTHING. Raised by the helper session of amend1015-face-migration Phase 1 (DECISIONs amend1015f D11 and D18). READ at `652029edc` and REPRODUCED with the real guard (Bubblewrap unreachable, fake builder and reviewer, `test_command="true"`, `repair_rounds=2`): `run_pingpong` ran 3 rounds, 2 of them repair rounds and 3 reviewer calls, and ended `repair_exhausted` with `tests_not_run` false, every round `test_passed` false at exit 126; with `repair_rounds=0` it ends `test_failed`. NEW: exec-1017c D7 made the guard's refusal carry `tripped_limit` `sandbox_unavailable`, and `failure_postmortem.classify` reads every tripped limit as `resource_limit`, so the refusal's post-mortem names a resource limit the guard never enforced. `job_verification` also reads a job whose only test check was refused, with no test request, as `not_required` instead of `not_run`. - R-1346 — High, exec-1018: A CANCELLED ORDER'S PLANNER CALL CANNOT BE INTERRUPTED, AND ITS FAILURE WRITES A FRESH FALLBACK PLAN. Raised by the same order, P0 3. SEARCHED BEFORE MINTING: R-1338 (order cancel) states the limit ("A planning order stops when its planner call in flight returns") as a decision, not a finding. READ at `652029edc`: `OllamaPlanner.raw_call` is one blocking `ollama.Client().chat()` with no stream, no timeout and no cancel hook, and the Claude CLI planner's `run_guarded` policy has no `cancel_check`, so a cancel waits for the whole generation. `compile_mission_plan` turns any exception of its call into the deterministic fallback and `plan_mission` writes it as a new plan version, so even an interrupted call would leave a plan written after the cancel. DECISION exec-1017c D3's "Remedy deliberately kills no process, as the kill switch does not" is false since amend1016sb: a job stop ends a running Pi call and its whole sandbox within about a second (`pi_provider._JOB_STOP_REQUESTED`, test `test_a_job_stop_ends_a_running_pi_call`). Owner: exec-1018. THE REPAIR: DECISION exec-1018 D2. RESOLVED in this branch.

Owner: exec-1018. THE REPAIR: DECISION exec-1018 D3. RESOLVED in this branch.

Make src/format.js handle negative numbers." is one deterministic task over three files, one builder call for three unrelated objectives and one review of all three as one; the lab's calc order of 2026-10-17 showed a local builder doing one of two named things. - R-1351 — Medium, exec-1018: A PROJECT TEST THAT GOES RED AFTER REVIEW ENDS THE JOB WITH NO REPAIR FROM THAT EVIDENCE. Raised by the order's local calibration (2026-10-18, case c2, the lab's add order verbatim, Pi over local Ollama `muse-glimmer:latest`, disposable repository): the builder wrote `import { test, strictEqual } from "node:test"` (`strictEqual` is `node:assert`'s), the reviewer passed the diff without running anything (its preamble tells it not to), and the DoD's real `npm test` failed with the SyntaxError; the job ended `blocked` (`dod_blocking_red`), verification `failed`, in 37 s and 28,419 tokens. Remedy told the truth, but the one concrete piece of evidence that would let a repair round fix a one-word import (the test output) reaches no model: the builder is told not to run tests, the loop's `--test-command` is not set for a `remedy do` job, and the DoD runs after the task passed. Not repaired in this branch: one real run is no measurement of a repair round's worth, and the candidates (the loop runs the project's test command before review; or one repair round of the last task when the DoD goes red) change `run_job`, which is at its ledger row. Owner: the next paydown, with an A/B over the lab orders.

- R-1352 — Low, exec-1018: A FULL-LANE ORDER ON A TINY REPOSITORY PAYS FOUR STUDY NARRATION CALLS. Raised by the same calibration (case c4, a six-file repository, a high-risk plan-only order): the study step made 4 `StudyCategoryNarration` planner calls (6.3 s, 5.8 s, 7.9 s, 3.8 s: 23.8 of the walk's 27.5 s) before the plan. `do_step_study` exempts only the bounded lane, although `settle_do_lane` computes `ctx.tiny_repo` (at most 10 tracked files) and `do_lane`'s module text promises deterministic study facts for a tiny repository; the planner's prompt carries the repository's facts block, which lists such a repository whole. The scale matrix shows the same 4 calls on its three tiny full-lane rows. Owner: exec-1018. THE REPAIR: DECISION exec-1018 D7. RESOLVED in this branch.

- R-1353 — Low, exec-1018: NO STORED LESSON OR STUDY CARD REACHES A MODEL CALL OF A `remedy do` WALK, YET A FULL-LANE WALK PAYS FOR THE CARDS. Raised by the order's lessons experiment (P1 9), deterministic, fake planner and providers recording every prompt: a first walk wrote 4 study narration cards; an approved lesson relevant to R-1351 ("strictEqual comes from node:assert, never node:test") and an irrelevant one were stored for the project through `packages.memory.local_gateway.store_memory`; the second walk's 3 planner, 2 builder and 2 reviewer prompts carried none of the six texts. The planner reads the repository facts block, the builder goal, file tree and named files; only `llm_planner.py` and `task_runner.py`, which the walk does not use, read memory. So the full lane's four narration calls (23.8 s locally, R-1352) buy cards that serve other surfaces (`remedy memory`, the project brain, readiness's `has_approved_memory`) and no decision of the walk. Not repaired: whether the walk should skip narration, or read lessons, is a product decision that changes readiness and the brain, and no measured run shows a lesson changing an outcome. Owner: the next paydown, as an A/B over the lab orders with a lesson block in the builder's context.
- R-1354 — Medium, exec-1018b: A FAILED TEST AFTER A PASSING REVIEW GIVES THE REPAIR ROUND NO EVIDENCE, EVEN WHERE A TASK HAS A TEST COMMAND. Raised by the R-1351 trace (operator order exec-1018b, 2026-10-18), deterministic: `make_repair_decision` answers `repair` (`test_failure_evidence`) when a task's tests fail, but with a reviewer that passed, the repair round's findings are the reviewer's empty list, and `compose_builder_prompt` renders the test result only `if test_result and findings`: the builder of the repair round receives its goal again with no failure, no finding and no test output. The round's summary also keeps only the last five output lines, which for `node --test` are its counters (`# skipped 0`, `# duration_ms ...`), never the error. Owner: this branch, with R-1351.
- R-1355 — Low, exec-1018b: A TEST SCRIPT WHOSE TOOL IS MISSING READS AS A FAILED TEST. Raised by the R-1351 trace: `dod_runners._spawn_check` judges any non-zero exit `nonzero_exit` with its exit code, so `npm test` whose script names a tool that is not installed (`sh: 1: jest: not found`, exit 127) is a red, failed test check, `job_verification` reads it `failed`, and a repair round fed that evidence would change application code for a host that ran no test. Owner: this branch, with R-1351.
- R-1356 — Low, exec-1018b: A SUPERVISED WALK'S ORDER OUTLIVES THE WALK IN ITS THREAD. Raised by the combined review of PRs 360 to 362 (operator order exec-1018b P0 3, the `_WALK_ORDER_DIR` named in PR 362's handback): `order_cancel.cancellable_do_steps` sets the `ContextVar` `_WALK_ORDER_DIR` when a walk starts and nothing resets it, so after a cancelled order's walk ended, the same thread's later `walk_cancel_request`, `walk_cancel_reader` and `walk_cancel_note` still answer that order's cancel: a planner call made through `intake.make_structured_call_fn` (mission plan, chat answer, summaries) would be bound to the old order and ended at once, and an apply would be refused. Demonstrated in one thread (red test `test_a_walks_order_ends_with_the_walk_and_binds_no_later_call_in_the_same_thread`). No production process is affected today (an order's walk runs in its own child, which exits; a thread starts with a fresh context), but tests and any embedded use of Remedy run walks in one long-lived thread. Owner: this branch.
- R-1357 — Medium, exec-1018b: A JOB CANCELLED WHILE ITS FINAL GATE RUNS ENDS COMPLETED AND IS APPLIED. Raised by the combined review of PRs 360 to 362 (cancellation races): `run_job`'s last safe point is after the last task; the job's DoD gate, which runs the project's tests and may take minutes, runs after it. `cancel_job` on the running job answers `cancel_pending: true` and writes the cancel and stop requests, but nothing reads them again: the job ends `completed` (`settle_job_cancel` then refuses a completed job), and a walk with `--apply` (or a commit flag) applies it through `do_apply_one_job`, which reads only the ORDER's cancel. Demonstrated by `test_a_cancel_during_the_jobs_final_gate_ends_it_cancelled_and_nothing_is_applied` (red: the job reads `completed` and README.md changed in the repository). Owner: this branch.
- R-1358 — Medium, exec-1018b: AN ORDER'S WORDING DECIDES WHETHER A FILE IT GUARDS IS CHANGED. Raised by the order's P1 4 (adversarial wordings, English and German): `order_intent._clause_role` reads every clause no pattern recognises as `required`, and a required file of a several-file task is both written into the builder's task ("Change each of them") and held to change (`named_files_unchanged`). Measured: of 24 adversarial wordings the pure reading misread 12 on PR 362's head ("without touching src/format.js", "src/format.js is off limits", "Finger weg von ...", "Read docs/FORMAT.md first", "is fine if it helps" all read required; "ohne etwas zu ändern" read as a change order; "Do not change any code" beside a documentation change held as a question), and 8 of 12 wordings written afterwards and never tuned on. End to end over a Node repository with its real `node --test` and a scripted correct builder, 9 of 21 cases failed on PR 362's head: correct work blocked because a context, optional or protected file stayed unchanged, a violated guard blocked for the wrong reason, the German verify-only order planned a change. `order_scope._sentence_actions` also counted a test run inside a sentence ("... und führe die Tests aus") as an action, so the German wording of an order took the full lane where the English one stayed bounded. Owner: this branch.
- R-1359 — High, exec-1018b: THE QUEUE OFFERS A BUDGET DECISION ITS ANSWER REFUSES, SO THE CLIENT'S QUESTION NEVER CLOSES. Raised by the operator on 2026-10-18 ("2 Fragen, die ich nicht beantworten kann, weil ich Fehlermeldungen bekomme"), read through the CLI on the live data root: jobs bd41e75f78b84b98 and 3806bb264aef46a5 are `blocked` because a task's Pi loop hit its cap (`task_..._gate_failed: final_status=budget_exhausted; missing_reviewer_output`); no budget guard stopped them. `decision_queue.list_decisions` offers a `token_budget` decision whenever the job's error text holds `budget_exhausted`, while `budget_decision.answer_budget_decision` accepts only `stop_source == "budget"` under the id `budget_decision_id(job.stop_request_id)`: every answer, `extend` or `abandon`, is refused `decision_not_found`, and Luna's "Aufgeben" of a budget question goes through that very route. The queue also took the decision id from the stop event, the answer from the job record. Owner: feature/close-from-chat.
- R-1360 — High, exec-1018b: NO CLIENT CAN TELL HOW TO CLOSE A JOB THAT IS STILL OPEN. Raised by the operator on 2026-10-18 ("einen der immer noch hängend läuft und 4 wartende, die ich auch nicht beenden kann. Das muss sich alles über den Chat schließen lassen können"): read through the CLI, four jobs are `stopped` and one never started (story `planning`), each with `next` `{action: none}`. The story names one step forward and never the way out, so a client has to guess which state takes `abandon` or `decline`. On the live stack (Remedy 1.17 on main, Luna's installed module) no route ends such a job at all (`job abandon` arrives with PR 361, 1.19); with it, the story still offered nothing. Owner: feature/close-from-chat.
- R-1361 — High, exec-1018c: ABANDONING AN ORDER'S JOB THAT A DEAD WALK LEFT `running` NEVER ENDS IT. A walk (`do_sequence`) runs its jobs in its own process, so an order's job has no run record; `remedy job abandon` (and `POST /api/v1/jobs/{job}/abandon`, the story's `end` since 1.21) read only the run record and took its absence for a live runner, so after a crash or supervisor restart the answer was `cancel_pending` true for a stop no process will ever read, and every repeat said the same: the job stayed `running` and open. `order_cancel._runner_alive` already read the walk. Reproduced red by `tests/orchestration/test_order_cancel.py::test_abandon_ends_at_once_an_order_job_its_ended_walk_left_running`; repaired by `serve_runs.job_runner_alive` (DECISION exec-1018c D1).
- R-1362 — High, exec-1018c: R-1359 RECURS THROUGH THE RUN LOG: A BUDGET STOP ANSWERED BEFORE A LATER STOP IS OFFERED AGAIN AND EVERY ANSWER REFUSED. PR 364 dropped the error text from `list_decisions`' budget trigger but kept three others its answer never takes: `"budget" in stop_source` or `budget_exhausted` in `stop_reason`, the legacy `budget_stop_reason` key (no writer left), and the first `job_stopped source=budget` event of the run log. A job stopped by its budget, answered `extend`, resumed and then stopped by the person (`stop_source` `api`) still had the old event, its new `stopped_at` postdated the answer, so the queue listed `budget:<new request>`, the story read `waiting` with `next: answer`, and `answer_budget_decision` refused `extend` and `abandon` with `decision_not_found`. Measured: 1,032 of 1,600 combinations of state, stop fields, legacy key and events offered a decision the answer refused; 0 after. Reproduced red by `tests/orchestration/test_budget_decision.py::test_a_budget_stop_answered_before_a_later_operator_stop_is_never_offered_again`; repaired by DECISION exec-1018c D2.
- R-1363 — Medium, exec-1018c: A REPAIR ROUND (OR ANY ROUND) MAY CHANGE A FILE A FENCED ORDER NEVER NAMED, AND THE JOB COMPLETES. The lab add order says "Ändere keine anderen Dateien"; `do_scope` fences only the planner's plan, and `deliverable_files_refusal` held a task only to its named files changing and its protected files staying; nothing deterministic read the fence against what a builder staged. Measured with PR 363's own walk: a repair answer that also rewrote `src/format.js` went green, the stand-in reviewer passed it, and the job ended `completed` with `src/format.js` waiting for the apply. Not new in the stack (a first round could do the same), but the order's P0 asks that repair respect the requested scope. A repair that rewrote the test script was already refused (`test_command_changed`). Reproduced red by `tests/cli/test_do_verified_repair.py::test_a_repair_round_stays_inside_the_files_the_order_names`; repaired by DECISION exec-1018c D3.
- R-1364 — Low, exec-1018c: THE REVIEW ZIP'S "NO REPORT EXISTS" NEVER SAID WHERE IT LOOKED. The installed supervisor (`luna-remedy-serve.service`: `remedy serve start` from `~/.local/bin`, no `REMEDY_DATA_DIR`, no `data_dir` in `~/.config/remedy/remedy.toml`) writes reports to the `.data/` of the checkout its editable package resolves to (`~/Repos/remedy`); `stage_review_reports.py` reads the `.data/` of the checkout it runs in. A ZIP built in a worktree therefore read an empty root and said only "no review report exists under the data root's review_reports/", the same words as a service with no capture: one plausible source of the earlier zero-report snapshot, which this session cannot verify (the live `.data` is not read). Every test set `REMEDY_DATA_DIR` for both sides, so none could see it. Repaired: `index.json` `reports_dir`, the note, `INDEX.md` and the script's output name the folder read (home as `~`); the doc says which checkout to build from (DECISION exec-1018c D4). The stale doc sentence that a waiting job run later is not in the report is corrected (it joins `continued`).
- R-1365 — Medium, rel-1019: A CANCEL ANSWERED `cancel_pending` WHOSE RUNNER DIED BEFORE ITS SAFE POINT LEFT THE JOB `running` AND ITS ORDER `cancelling` FOR GOOD. Reproduced on PR 365's head `7181a2311` with a real fake-provider walk: an order cancelled while its walk ran a job answers `cancelling`/`cancel_pending` (correct: the walk may still hold the job); when the walk's process then ends without reaching its safe point (a crash, an out-of-memory kill, a reboot), nothing ever carries out the request: the job's record stays `running`, `GET /api/v1/orders/{order}` reads `lost`/`cancelling` on every poll, and only a second cancel or abandon (which a client following "poll until cancelled" never sends) ends it. The same holds for a supervisor run (`POST /api/v1/jobs/{job}/run`) that dies while the supervisor lives: its reaper wrote the run's end and nothing else. Reproduced red by `tests/orchestration/test_order_cancel.py::test_an_order_cancelled_while_its_job_runs_ends_when_its_walk_dies_before_the_safe_point` and three tests of `tests/orchestration/test_serve_runs.py` and `tests/orchestration/test_serve_daemon.py`; repaired by DECISION rel-1019 D2. Owner: feature/release-readiness.
- R-1366 — Medium, rel-1019: THE STORY OFFERS `run` FOR A JOB WHOSE PLAN THE PERSON REJECTED, AND EVERY RUN REFUSES IT. A task plan waiting for approval reads `waiting`/`task_plan_approval` with `next: answer` (correct). Answered `reject` through that very route, the job stays `planned` with `task_plan._approval: rejected`, which `job_plan.task_plan_blocks_execution` makes final for the job (`REJECTED_PLAN_NEXT_STEP`: give the order anew); the story then read `queued` with `next: run`, and `POST /api/v1/jobs/{job}/run` starts a run that `checkpoints` refuses with `plan_rejected`, so a client button loops; the order's view still read `needs_plan_approval` although nothing is left to approve. Same class as R-1359/R-1362 (an offered action its route refuses). Reproduced red by `tests/ui_server/test_public_api_lifecycle.py::test_a_rejected_plan_offers_no_run_and_ends_through_its_end`; repaired by DECISION rel-1019 D3. Owner: feature/release-readiness.
- R-1367 — Low, rel-1019: A CHANGE REMEDY'S OWN FILE CHECK REFUSED READS `unknown` IN THE STORY, WHILE THE 1.21 RELEASE MATRIX SAYS `review_not_passed`. `docs/system/api-release-matrix-v1.json` (exec-1018c, R-1363's value change) states the story of a fenced order's refused round reads `failed` with reason `review_not_passed` "as protected_file_changed already reads"; no test asserted it, and `client_story._failure_reason` reads neither `unrequested_file_changed` nor `protected_file_changed` nor `named_files_unchanged` (the task's `final_status` is `staged_review_passed`, the refusal is only in its `error`), so all three read `unknown`. Measured with the verified-repair walk: fence and test-script-rewrite cases read `unknown`. Reproduced red by `tests/cli/test_do_verified_repair.py::test_a_refused_repair_reads_review_not_passed_in_the_story`; repaired by DECISION rel-1019 D4. Owner: feature/release-readiness.
- R-1368 — Medium, agent-data-hygiene review: A STAGED SYMLINK UNDER `.agent` BYPASSED THE NEW CONTENT GUARD. The text-diff scanner rejected binary members and sensitive added lines but treated a relative symlink target such as a parent traversal as harmless text. A coding agent could follow that link to data outside `.agent`, defeating the directory boundary even though review ZIP discovery prunes `.agent` safely. Reproduced with a temporary repository and repaired by comparing every changed `.agent` path with its staged Git mode, allowing only regular files; Git text conversion and external diff drivers are disabled for the content scan. Owner: feature/agent-data-hygiene.
- R-1369 — High, amend1020-orb-insight: AN ORDER THAT NAMES NO FILE FAILS BEFORE ANY JOB, BECAUSE THE TASK-PLAN GUARD READS A PLANNER-WRITTEN OUTLINE AS THE ORDER. Raised by the operator's second lab run of 2026-10-20 (Remedy 1.21, Pi over the local model Muse Glimmer, review report `rr-20261020T130335Z`, order `4a4af8f164bb4d659b5634a8e1d66f13`): "Super, jetzt mach ein schickes design" ended `failed` after five planner calls, 226 s and 7,423 estimated tokens with no job: "task plan rejected: every task delivers a file the order did not ask for (T002, T003)". The order names no file and draws no fence; for such an order the planner's files are the delivery. READ at `8ee02682d`: for the person's order `do_scope.order_has_scope_fence` is false and `order_scope_files` is empty, so `out_of_scope_task_ids` would refuse nothing; the walk took the `milestones` shape, and `_step_shape` hands `plan_order_job` each milestone's planner-written job outline as its `order`, against which `_deliverable_task_plan` judged the task plan. Owner: F312.
- R-1370 — Medium, amend1020-orb-insight: ONE BUDGET STOP ASKS THE OPERATOR TWO QUESTIONS. Raised by the operator's third lab run of 2026-10-20 (report `rr-20261020T143546Z`, job `48ce0648d73747eb`, `budget_exhausted:max_wall_clock_minutes`): the stop raised the budget decision and, because the mission's contract had blockers, the remainder decision `td:d39ea71e` at the same time, and the Orb sent a notification for each. READ at `8ee02682d`: `do_sequence.do_budget_stop_remainder` raises the remainder decision for any job whose `stop_source` is budget while blockers are open, without regard to the open budget decision that may still extend the job; `mission_contract.answer_contract_remainder_on_extend` (F295 D19) then has to close it again on `extend`. Owner: F310.
- R-1371 — Medium, amend1020-orb-insight: THE WALL CLOCK COUNTS WAITING AS WORK, AND ON A LOCAL MODEL ITS LIMIT ENDS NORMAL WORK. Raised by the same run: the job was stopped after 9 minutes in the middle of its work (78,696 tokens, 3 calls, 12 builder turns), with a planner call taking 40 to 76 s on Muse Glimmer; the continue at 15:02:53 ended `stopped` again at once because the limit had not been raised. READ at `8ee02682d`: `budget_guard` measures `max_wall_clock_minutes` from the job's start (`budget_guard.py:326`, `:417`), so minutes spent waiting for a decision, a lock or a model server count against it, and the default limits are one set for every provider (`budget.*`, filled by the task plan's own `budgets`, `do_sequence.py:518`). Owner: F310.

- R-1372 — Low, F310 R2: NO TEST READS THE ACTIVE MOMENT `run_job` SAVES, SO A WRONG ONE WOULD COUNT A RUN'S OWN WORK AS WAITING AT THE NEXT RESUME. Raised by the reviewer's mutation at the F310 R2 gate. SEARCHED BEFORE MINTING (checklist item 30): the ledger and its archive for `active_until`, a field this feature added; no entry names it. READ at `e8924be11`: `_persist_budget_actuals` in `run_job` writes `"active_until": datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat()`; replacing it with the first run's instant left `tests/orchestration/test_budget_clock.py`, `tests/orchestration/test_budget_guard.py`, `tests/orchestration/test_pi_timeouts.py` and `tests/orchestration/test_budget_profiles.py` green at 179 passed, because every resume test builds its record by hand and none reads back what a run saved. A wrong value would make the next resume count every second since the first run as waiting, so the wall clock would never stop a long job. THE REPAIR: a test that runs a job and asserts that the saved record's `active_until` lies inside that run, and that a resume an hour later adds about an hour to `waited_s`. Owner: F310.

Landed: R-1372 — a test in the clock tests runs the nine-hour local job, reads the saved record's active_until inside the run and a resume an hour later adding 3,600 seconds to waited_s, in the commit "F310 R3 C2: a run's saved active moment is read back (R-1372)".

- R-1373 — Low, F310 R3: NO TEST SEES THE DOUBLING COUNT A JOB'S WAITING AS WORK. Raised by the reviewer's mutation at the F310 R3 gate. SEARCHED BEFORE MINTING (checklist item 30): the ledger and its archive for `doubled_limit_answers` and `_work_seconds`, both added by this feature; no entry names them. READ at `79ed24137`: `_work_seconds` in `packages/orchestration/budget_stop.py` subtracts the record's `waited_s` from the time between its start and its last active moment; dropping that subtraction left `tests/orchestration/test_budget_stop.py` and the five other targeted files green at 480 passed, because no test of the doubling uses a job that waited. With the subtraction gone, a job stopped on its calls after waiting hours for an earlier answer would have its wall clock doubled as well, although its work never reached that limit. THE REPAIR: a test of `doubled_limit_answers` for a job whose saved record waited longer than its wall-clock limit while its work stayed far below it, stopped on another limit: the answer doubles that limit and not the wall clock. - R-1385 — Low, F311 R2: A JOB THAT WAITS BEHIND ITS ORDER'S APPLY READS `apply_wait`. Raised by the reviewer at the F311 R2 gate. DECISION F311 D3 gives `apply_wait` to "a job whose order phase is `awaiting_apply`", and `job_insight_entry` at `105378025` implements that wording. - R-1321 — Medium, F078: THE RECORD OF LEVEL CHANGES IS KEYED BY THE CONFIG PATH AS WRITTEN, NOT AS RESOLVED, SO EVERY PROJECT'S `remedy.toml` SHARES ONE KEY. Raised by the reviewer at F078's round 1 gate. SEARCHED BEFORE MINTING (checklist item 30): the ledger and its archive for `level_changes`, `config_path` and `autonomy_gate`; no entry names them. READ at `4b72b06d5`: `load_autonomy_policy` takes `path` from `config.load_report.project_path` or `user_path` as the report holds it and uses that string as the key of `<data root>/autonomy/level_changes.jsonl` and as `AutonomyPolicy.config_path`. `load_config()` without arguments records the project path as the relative `remedy.toml` (`_DEFAULT_PROJECT_PATH`), so two projects in two folders both write and read the key `remedy.toml`. THE EFFECT: once one project has recorded a change to `L3` with its decision record, a second project that sets `L3` with passing gauntlet evidence and no decision record reads `L3` as its previous level, sees no change and is accepted without one, which is the refusal DECISION F078 D1 (4) exists for; and a downgrade in one project reads as a change in the other. The block's C3 (2)(e) ordered the key to be "the level's resolved config file", and DECISION F078 D1 alternative (c) rejects exactly a shared key. NO TEST SAW IT because every test passes an absolute path under `tmp_path`. A SECOND, SMALLER DEFECT IN THE SAME READER: gauntlet evidence that is not valid UTF-8 raises `UnicodeDecodeError` out of `load_autonomy_policy` instead of the authored refusal "is not a gauntlet matrix Remedy can read". THE FIX: key the record, and fill `config_path`, with the resolved absolute path of the level's config file, resolve both supporting paths to absolute paths, and refuse unreadable evidence (`OSError` or `ValueError` on reading or parsing) with the authored sentence; each with a test that is red without it — two projects in two folders loaded with the relative default path, and evidence holding bytes that are not UTF-8. OPEN.

SEARCHED BEFORE MINTING (checklist item 30): the ledger for `scan_staged_diff` and `+++`; R-1368 hardened the check's rules and R-1376 names the rotation, neither this. MEASURED at `eeed13064`: `scan_staged_diff` of `scripts/check_agent_hygiene.py` takes every line that starts with `+++ ` as the name of the next file, inside a hunk too, so a staged `.agent/plan.md` whose first added line is two plus signs, a space and a home-directory path, and whose second sets an API key, reads as no finding at all: the first line renames the file scanned to a path outside `.agent/`, and every later added line of that file goes unscanned. - R-1413 — Medium, F307 R7: `load_job_plan_safe` RAISES ON A JOB RECORD WHOSE TASK LIST IS NOT A LIST OF OBJECTS, ALTHOUGH ITS DOCSTRING SAYS NO INPUT MAKES IT RAISE. Raised by the reviewer while preparing F307's round 7. SEARCHED BEFORE MINTING (checklist item 30): the ledger for `load_job_plan_safe` and `AttributeError`; DECISION F275 D23 made the loader, and no entry names this. MEASURED at `d9f3c26bc` with a probe of thirteen damaged records: a record whose `tasks` is a string, or a list of numbers, makes `_import_job` raise `AttributeError`, which neither `load_job_plan` nor `load_job_plan_safe` catches, so every reader that trusts the safe loader to answer `(None, True)` for a damaged record crashes instead; none of the probe's other damages, an unknown state among them, made either loader raise. THE REPAIR: the safe loader catches `AttributeError` too, and the failure path of an order's planning reads its jobs with it. Owner: F307, repaired in the same round as R-1311's failure path by DECISION F307 D8.
